# Merging Offline and Online User Data

## 

Overview

Journey maps allow you to connect online and offline user data to create a seamless experience for your customers. You can upload offline events that occurred in your physical stores, such as purchases and membership registrations, and use these events in journey maps.

See Use case: encourage offline purchase for an example.

### 

Requirements

In journey maps, users across different channels are unified based on `user_id`. To learn more about the requirements and limitations of using offline events in journey maps, see the requirements for [offline events and segments](doc:getting-started-with-journey-maps#offline-events-and-segments).

* * *

## 

Best practices

Follow the best practices below to ensure offline events are effectively uploaded and used in journey maps.

  * Upload events frequently
  * Add an upload time interval



### 

Upload events frequently

Unlike online events tracked via Appier SDK, offline events need to be uploaded to AIQUA via APIs. As a result, there will always be a time gap between when the event actually occurred and when the event is uploaded.

We recommend uploading offline events at least once a day if you want to use offline events or segments in journey maps.

### 

Add an upload time interval to journey flow

In some nodes, you can extend the time periods to allow more time for offline events to be uploaded.

  * **Past condition** : Add the upload time interval to the time period you want to set in **Only consider events in the last x days**. For example, if your event upload interval is one day and you want to consider events in the last 3 days, set the **Only consider events in the last x days** settings to four days.

![](https://files.readme.io/88640d64f632aa7d5cdf9b563321dc68e682cc456b783f31a697b6067e9dc1fb-consider-events.png)

  * **Real-time event** : The event timestamp must be later than the journey activation time, and the users will enter the journey after the offline events are uploaded.
  * **Wait for conditions:** Extend the timeout period by adding the event upload interval. For example, if your event upload interval is one day, and you want to wait seven days for users to complete the conditions, set the timeout period to eight days.

![](https://files.readme.io/1351500-Screen_Shot_2023-11-27_at_6.05.08_PM.png)

  * **Check condition** : Insert a time delay node before the check condition node to make sure offline events are already uploaded before users proceed to the check condition node. For example, if you upload offline events once a day, you can add a time delay node to put users on hold for a day.

![](https://files.readme.io/1f6d30b-check_condition_time_delay.png)

  * **Goal event** : Goal events are calculated based on the actual timestamp of the offline events. The event count will be added after the event upload, but the end results are not affected.
  * **Exit rule** : Users who meet the exit rule will exit the journey after the exit events are uploaded.



* * *

## 

Use case: Encourage offline purchases

Let's say you want to send marketing campaigns to the VIP members to encourage offline purchases in your physical stores.

First, make sure offline purchase events are uploaded to AIQUA daily.

Next, set up the journey map as below.

  1. In the entry trigger node, select **Past condition** and set the condition to include VIP members.
  2. Add a **Wait for condition** node and add the offline purchase event as the condition.
  3. In the **Matched** path, add a thank-you email to users who have completed offline purchases.
  4. In the **Timeout** path, email encourage offline purchases.
  5. If the email is opened, send another email to follow up. If the email is not opened, send a message through SMS instead.
  6. Set the offline purchase event as the goal event and exit rule.

![](https://files.readme.io/b30cd8d4fb3d8e254b8934ec670e1dc611421b12eaca7d7a027609c186cdd888-offline-purchase.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

# Message Node

Message nodes allow you to reach out to users across all your integrated channels. Depending on the channel you select, you can either add a creative or a chatbot flow module.

  * Add a message node
  * Set the split criteria



* * *

## 

Add a message node

Click on any **+** icon that appears between and after nodes, then click **Channels** , and select the channel you'd like to use to send the message.

![](https://files.readme.io/c97e268-message-node-channel.png)

Next, enter a node name and set up the creatives or flow modules, depending on whether you selected a message node for an AIQUA channel or BotBonnie channel.

### 

Message node: AIQUA channel

If you add a message node for an AIQUA channel, you can set up the by clicking **\+ Add creative**.

> 👍
> 
>   * To learn more about each creative and its settings, see [Creatives](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/creatives#/).
>   * Send time optimization is supported for push and email.
> 


![](https://files.readme.io/58f6f75-message-node-aq-channel.png)

To view more creative templates for app push channels (Android and iOS), check **Use advanced Android template** or **Use advanced iOS template**.

![](https://files.readme.io/a439f5a-push-advanced-templates.png)

#### 

Send time optimization

Send time optimization uses Appier's AI to determine the most optimal send time in the 24-hour period following the campaign's scheduled send time to maximize engagement, and is available for push and email campaigns. The 24-hour sending window is based on your account's [time zone setting](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/user-settings#timezone).

  * **Personalized targeting** : The actual send time is uniquely optimized for each user, considering individual behavior and overall engagement trends to determine the optimal send time.
  * **Real-time learning** : Appier's AI continually learns and updates its definition of what's optimal based on new engagement data.
  * **Built-in safeguards to reduce bias** : Unlike simpler rule-based systems, Appier's send time optimization ensures training data isn’t skewed by past, manually scheduled campaigns. In addition, self-balancing systems prevent feedback loops caused by the AI training on its own past behavior. These safeguards ensure optimization decisions are based solely on user behavior.



To enable send time optimization for the node, check **Enable send time optimization**.

![](https://files.readme.io/d429799742e858b9b2a35d208de10bc9e54fb01a06bdcbd8f544b70a25856069-message-node-sto.png)

Note that when send time optimization is enabled:

  * If no reference data is available, the campaign is sent immediately.
  * The timeout to wait for the message to be sent, delivered, opened, or clicked is automatically extended to account for the actual send time.



### 

Message node: BotBonnie channel

If you add a message node for a BotBonnie channel, you'll see a button to add a flow module.

> 👍
> 
> To learn more about creating modules, see [Modules and Messages](https://docs.botbonnie.appier.com/docs/modules-and-messages).

![](https://files.readme.io/14f5c9b-message-node-bb-channel.png)

* * *

### 

Using dynamic content in message node

Dynamic content in message nodes enables you to create personalized interactions by customizing messages based on available user data. To insert personalized content, click the **{⋯}** icon in supported creative fields.

![](https://files.readme.io/1f85108-jm_dynamic_content.png)

#### 

Supported channels and data sources

Data availability depends on the source of the channel's dynamic content. Refer to the tables below for data sources supported in AIQUA and BotBonnie channels.

##### 

AIQUA channels

The following table shows which data types are supported for each campaign type in AIQUA channels.

Channel| Attributes & events| [Recommendation](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/recommendations-v2)| Feed trigger  
---|---|---|---  
Push| Yes| Yes| No  
SMS| Yes| Yes| No  
LMS (South Korea only)| Yes| Yes| No  
Email| Yes| Yes| No  
In-web| No| No| No  
In-app| No| No| No  
  
##### 

BotBonnie channels

The following table shows which data types are supported for each campaign type in BotBonnie channels. Only data collected through BotBonnie is available for dynamic content in BotBonnie channels.

Channel| Attributes & events| Recommendation| Feed Trigger  
---|---|---|---  
LINE| Yes| No| No  
Viber| Yes| No| No  
Zalo| Yes| No| No  
Whatsapp| Yes| No| No  
WebChat| Yes| No| No  
Kakao| No| No| No  
  
For all [BotBonnie channels](doc:common-settings-channels), you can use these data types:

  * **User demographics** collected through BotBonnie.

  * **Custom parameters** created in BotBonnie.

  * **User events** , including:

    * Cart items (from `product_added_to_cart` events)
    * Browsed items (from `product_viewed` events)
    * Purchased items (from `product_purchased` events)

![](https://files.readme.io/7e2d0cf2b3a0b6ee614494d37b3e476c112c05c3b4a0c399ab6e8d852dac11f3-whitelisted-user-event.png)

* * *

## 

Set the split criteria

Users who receive the message will be split into two paths depending on whether the message was sent, delivered, clicked, or opened. You can click the dropdown menu to change the split criteria.

![](https://files.readme.io/b47a66f9fd72652703f99fd3165868ee132bd91a8891c0c8756b634cfd2a0ae7-setting-split-criteria.png)

# Audience Sync Node

Sync audience data with Meta Ads

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Beta feature
> 
> The audience sync node is a beta feature. Contact your customer success manager to learn more.

The audience sync node allows seamless synchronization of audience data across advertising platforms—particularly valuable in a cookieless environment, empowering marketers to leverage first-party data for more precise and effective targeting. Key benefits include:

  * **Retargeting** : Utilize first-party data to retarget users across platforms, maintaining engagement and driving conversions even without cookies.
  * **Purchase suppression** : Optimize ad spend by excluding users who have already made a purchase, ensuring ads are directed at potential new customers.
  * **Lookalike audiences** : Create lookalike audiences to attract new users with similar characteristics to your existing high-value customers.



This guide contains the following topics:

  1. Audience sync node usage details: Specific details on prerequisites, behavioral notes, limitations, and suggested best practices
  2. Audience sync setup
     1. Adding Appier as a Meta business partner: Enable Appier's integration with Meta ads.
     2. Add an audience sync node in the journey: Set up an audience sync node in your journey map.



* * *

## 

Audience sync node usage details

### 

Notes and limitations

  * When you make a major journey structure change (e.g. remove node), the performance of the journey node will be affected as well. The new number will be based on the state of the journey, not all the data points from the beginning journey.
  * Users sent to the ads platform do not persist in AIQUA's or AIRIS's database, and are not available as segments.
  * If there are network or connection issues with the connected ads platform, the audience sync node will be skipped, and the user will automatically be moved to the next node.
  * If the ads platform connection is deleted, the audience sync node will be skipped. The rest of the journey won't be affected.



### 

Best practices

  * **Batch processing** : For optimal performance, avoid passing large segments (greater than 10,000 users) of users to the journey node at once.
  * **Privacy compliance** : When building audiences, include or exclude users based on preferences and privacy laws. Implement relevant filters for users' eligibility with user segmentation.
  * **Understand the customer journey** : Understanding the customer journey is essential for creating an effective journey map. Identify key touchpoints and interactions to align your audience sync strategy with the customer lifecycle. This alignment ensures your synced audience matches your marketing funnel, delivering the right message at the right time and enhancing the overall customer experience.



* * *

## 

Audience sync setup

### 

1\. Add Appier as a Meta business partner

Follow these steps to add Appier as a Business Partner in your Meta Business Suite account or your Meta Business Manager account.

  1. Log in to your Meta Business Suite account or Meta Business Manager account.
  2. Go to the Business Settings page.
     * If you are using Meta Business Suite, click **Settings** in the left menu, and select **More business settings**.

![](https://files.readme.io/0a02e9adb25ac6ff327c763fd5151fd888c86e4053788d053e35598be0c8df27-add-meta-business-partner-1.png)

  * If you are using Meta Business Manager, click the Business Tools icon and select **Business settings**.

![](https://files.readme.io/3fec1d9bd5e597471acfbb0c3fbdac64f25dcac3b6e2a75976c3f60095e3be15-add-meta-business-partner-2.png)

  3. In the left menu of the Business Settings page, select **Ad Accounts**.

![](https://files.readme.io/05d21e0520d8b6460edd001870771c2588bde2b006eddaab742867e2411f0a3a-add-meta-business-partner-3.png)

  4. Select the ad account you want to export to, and click the **Assign Partners** button.

![](https://files.readme.io/9b2f175c501878f374f0b3083f3157558e31cdb3a69013f3ca2a8cc34d177308-add-meta-business-partner-4.png)

  5. Select **Business ID** and enter Appier's business ID: **1546643532281115**.
  6. Under Admin Access, allow access to **Manage Ad Account** and click **Next**.

![](https://files.readme.io/1b8760f-facebook_business.png)

  7. Go to Meta's **Custom Audiences Terms** page via the link below and click **Accept**.  
Change [Ad_Account_ID] to the actual account ID of your ad account: `www.facebook.com/ads/manage/customaudiences/tos/?act=[Ad_Account_ID]`

![](https://files.readme.io/57d7943-Facebook_terms.png)

### 

2\. Add a Meta Ads connection in the Appier console

  1. Form the project launcher in the top left of the navigation bar, go to **Common settings** , then go to **Ad platforms**. Under **Meta Ads** , click **Connect an account**.

![](https://files.readme.io/524711207893f88c229a9d02105b2e59ba62b28b92afd00aef3f117664a937ce-audience-sync-common-settings-ad-platforms.png)
  2. Authorize Appier to access the ad accounts you'd like to sync audiences for. You can select multiple ad accounts.


![](https://files.readme.io/774548b-c5a138e-image.png)

After completing the authorization process, you'll be able to see the connected account in the Appier console under **Integrated accounts**.

![](https://files.readme.io/b550c5a-image.png)   


### 

3\. Add an audience sync node in the journey

  1. In the journey map, add an audience sync node. Click on any **+** button, then click **Audience sync**.

![](https://files.readme.io/00e95f4-image.png)

  2. Click **\+ Add platform** and select the ad platforms you'd like to sync audiences for.

![](https://files.readme.io/f10ca19-image.png)

  3. Click the dropdown under **Audience** and choose whether you'd like to create a new audience list or reuse an existing one.

![](https://files.readme.io/6ea9a84-image.png)

To create a new custom audience list, enter a name and description, then click **Create ad audience**.

![](https://files.readme.io/b98cca9-image.png)

  4. Under **Action** , choose whether this node should add or remove users from the audience list.

![](https://files.readme.io/13426e7-image.png)

  5. Under **Audience match criteria** , select one ore more unique identifiers that should be used to match users with.
     1. For instance, if you opt for both **Email** and **Phone** , the system will send both identifiers to the ad platform for user matching. The user is added to the custom audience list on the ad platform if any of these identifiers match.



> 👍
> 
> To ensure data security, Appier only transmits hashed phone numbers and emails to connected ads platform.

![](https://files.readme.io/d15cc02-image.png)

### 

4\. Review audience sync performance

Once the journey map is published, you can review the performance of each node, including the audience sync node by clicking on the node. From the node status, you can see how many users have entered the node, as well as how many users have been successfully added to the custom audience list in the connected ad platform.

Note that the number of users displayed as **Added** or **Removed** in the audience sync node might not match the number on the connected ad platform exactly. This is because **Added** and **Removed** counts represent the number of users successfully matched from the connected ad platform, not the current custom audience size on the ads platform.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> The **Added** or **Removed** count won't always update immediately, and may require some time to sync with the data from the ads platform.

![](https://files.readme.io/e770e85-image.png)

You can also see the daily audience sync trend from the **Node stats** tab:

![](https://files.readme.io/8ebf1b0-image.png)

__Updated 11 days ago

# Deliver personalized onboarding journeys

## 

Background

A user visiting your website is just the initial step in the customer journey. To truly convert visitors into valuable customers for your brand, it's imperative to provide personalized engagement at the right moment. This personalized approach ensures that customers feel valued and understood, increasing the likelihood of conversion and fostering long-term brand loyalty.

### 

Objective

How can I provide personalized onboarding journeys to new visitors?

### 

Challenges

  * Identifying the right timing to engage with new users based on their real-time behavior.
  * Inability to offer a scalable cross-channel onboarding journey.



* * *

## 

Appier's solution

  * **Products** : AIQUA + BotBonnie
  * **Feature** : Journey map
    * Appier's journey maps utilize an omni-channel customer journey builder, enabling you to quickly launch personalized, comprehensive campaigns across Appier's supported marketing channels.
  * **Strengths** :
    * Enables comprehensive cross-channel user journeys, allowing for personalized engagement based on individual user behavior and preferences.
    * Seamlessly integrates user data across various channels to provide a holistic, 360-degree customer view of user profiles, allowing for more targeted engagement strategies.
    * Provides a user-friendly drag-and-drop editor that facilitates intuitive customization without the need for coding expertise.

![](https://files.readme.io/29ed728-journey-map.jpg)

* * *

## 

Setup guide

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> To learn more about different ways to create journey maps, and how to configure settings in detail, see [Creating a Journey Map](doc:creating-a-journey-map).

### 

1\. Create a "New user registration" journey map

From the navigation sidebar, click **Journey maps** > **\+ Create journey map**.

![](https://files.readme.io/23712267810ed72b0fda3c6c9b38821e8033b5365dbe777f1b55ef4b81d1d112-covert-journey-map.png)

Next, [choose a journey creation method](doc:choose-a-journey-creation-method#1-choose-a-creation-method) by selecting the **New User Registration** template.

![](https://files.readme.io/c282e81-new-user-registration-journey.png)

### 

2\. Customize the "New user registration" journey map

  1. Set an entry point. In this example, we select **Past condition** to exclude users who have registered within the last 180 days.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Logging events
> 
> To complete this node's setting, you’ll need to log a user registration event from your website in advance. For more information on how to log user events on your website, please refer to [Logging User Data for Web](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/logging-data-on-the-web-sdk).

![](https://files.readme.io/d592000-new-user-registration-entry-point.png) ![](https://files.readme.io/df7f14f-new-user-registration-past-condition.png)   


  2. Set up goal events for performance tracking. In this example, we set `account_registered` as the goal event and exit rule, allowing users to exit the journey after registering an account.

![](https://files.readme.io/1196165-new-user-registration-goals-and-exit-rule.png)

  3. Finally, customize the messages in the nodes for each channel. For instance, begin with a warm welcome message followed by a promotion highlighting the benefits of registering to encourage users to join as members.

![](https://files.readme.io/0a007aa-new-user-registration-customized.png)

### 

3\. Create a "Welcome new members" journey map

Once the user has successfully registered, you might want to deliver a welcome journey to educate the newly registered user about your brand and products to drive the first conversion.

From the left navigation bar, click **Journey maps** , then click **\+ Create journey map**.

![](https://files.readme.io/23712267810ed72b0fda3c6c9b38821e8033b5365dbe777f1b55ef4b81d1d112-covert-journey-map.png)

Next, select the **Welcome new members** template.

![](https://files.readme.io/4e52c37-welcome-new-members-template.png)

### 

4\. Customize the "Welcome new members" journey map

  1. Set an entry point. In this example, we modify the template's entry trigger to a **Past condition** that only allows users who registered in the last 10 days to enter the journey.

![](https://files.readme.io/4ee3b1f-welcome-new-members-replace-trigger.png) ![](https://files.readme.io/fe6c4ef-welcome-new-members-entry-settings.png)

  2. Set up goal events for performance tracking. In this journey, the goal events used for performance tracking are `checkout_completed` and `product_added_to_cart`. The exit rule is based on the `checkout_completed` events, so users exit the journey after completing a checkout.

![](https://files.readme.io/e327184-welcome-new-members-goal-exit-rules.png)

  3. Finally, customize the messages in the nodes for each channel. For instance, begin with a welcome email followed by an in-web pop-up campaign highlighting key product features. Enhance the journey further by adding messages such as product recommendations, first-purchase incentives, or pop-up sales promotions.

![](https://files.readme.io/e071e1c-newly-registered-journey.png)

### 

5\. Track each journey's performance

Appier’s journey maps provide comprehensive performance metrics and analytics that enable you to understand the effectiveness of your journeys. To learn more, see [Journey Performance and Analytics](doc:journey-performance-and-analytics).

# Identify the most effective acquisition channel

## 

Background

To effectively reach potential users, brands must utilize multiple channels, including email, SMS, paid ads (including Google and Facebook), and social media (including Instagram and LINE). However, data across these channels is often scattered and not consolidated, making it challenging for marketers to evaluate each channel's effectiveness.

### 

Objective

How do I identify the most effective channels to acquire new users?

### 

Challenges

  * Lack of a centralized source for analyzing and comparing acquisition channels.
  * Creating reports manually is time-consuming, and reports must be recreated whenever new data is available.



* * *

## 

Appier's solution

  * **Product** : AIRIS
  * **Feature** : [Attribution](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/attribution) report
    * AIRIS's attribution reports allow marketers to easily quantify the effectiveness of various touchpoints and campaigns leading to conversions. Marketers can then leverage these insights to create segments for further retargeting efforts.
  * **Strengths** :
    * Report data visualizations enable easy analysis of cross-channel acquisition performance.
    * Automatically updated data ensures accurate and up-to-date performance tracking.
    * Segments can be easily leveraged across various integrated platforms for targeted marketing efforts.

![](https://files.readme.io/106650d-target-audience.jpg)

* * *

## 

Setup guide

### 

1\. Create a new attribution report

From navigation sidebar, go to **Analyze** , then click **\+ New Report > Attribution**.

![](https://files.readme.io/110b60573c3763ba7df1116104de826cf08ae179617ea58f5c29b1e58c73c430-use-case-create-attribution-report.png)

### 

2\. Configure report settings

In this example, we want to assess how many website visitors in the past year from each acquisition channel (such as Google, Facebook) have converted into registered members. To do so, we'll configure the following settings:

  * Timeframe
  * Performed by
  * Initial Events
  * Touchpoints
  * Conversion

![](https://files.readme.io/bf0c649708a68829f5b9efe1df21ae94631aff78a32242fb5680efabb0f3b11a-image.png)

#### 

Timeframe

If a user performed at least one event in the specified timeframe, their data will be included in this report. In this example, we’ve selected **1 year ago**.

![](https://files.readme.io/cd92fd4d11a726434ab071317d4b5f67f2255b9e130f81678ccec28e71caaf3d-timeframe.png)

#### 

Performed by

Determines the segment or group of people you'll be analyzing. In this example, we would like to analyze all users in the database, so we’ll select **All users**.

![](https://files.readme.io/03b8e8d0eb6173d0b26a8ae159a56999301fe60b9a15191ef06392e68ee6b24d-performed-by.png)

#### 

Initial Events

Select the first event (or starting point) where you want the attribution analysis to begin within the selected timeframe.

In this example, we would like to include all the users who have ever viewed any page on the website (for example, all the visitors), so we’ll select the **Page view** event without adding any additional constraints.

![](https://files.readme.io/a96579f9b86b2168926c7bd356218996e7351bcbb367a37f1e496974bc546184-initial-events.png)

#### 

Touchpoints

Touchpoints are the specific attribution events that you want to analyze. In this example, we'd like to analyze the difference between each acquisition channel, so we’ve chosen the **UTM campaign** as the touchpoint and selected **Page view** where **Campaign Source = exists** , where the campaign source is where the ad was displayed, for example, Google, Facebook, Instagram.

The dimension determines how the report is organized. For example, since we’ve selected **Campaign Source** as the dimension, the report will display an analysis of all the different campaign sources.

![](https://files.readme.io/6cd3e8127959d14420c517f660894493d9c52f82e55995d800caeb5fcba97b46-touchpoints.png)

#### 

Conversion

This is the conversion event that you want to analyze. In this example, we’ve chosen **Register member** as the conversion event.

![](https://files.readme.io/e5231ab0b53b9fe50e9bb1b5aa5631ef236c581295b41fde791ab02609a892f2-conversion-events.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Developer assistance will be required to log custom events (for example, “Register member”) using the Appier SDK. To learn more, see [Custom Events and Attributes](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/custom-user-data) .

### 

3\. Select an attribution model

Select the attribution model that you want to use. The model determines how credit is assigned for the conversion.

In this example, we’ve selected **Last Touch** , where the last touchpoint receives 100% of the credit for the conversion.

![](https://files.readme.io/cbe0287458c5388a964c8e93b80870bad662990aa0d138c7bb6ee38f2603fa8a-model.png)

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Attribution
> 
> To learn more about the available attribution models, refer to [Attribution report guide](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/attribution#model) .

### 

4\. Run the report

We can see in the report that among all the 224 conversions, `google` was the highest-performing campaign channel, resulting in 62 conversions—meaning users registered as members.

The report also indicates the average time taken from the initial event (page viewed) to conversion (registered as a member). Interestingly, the data reveals that the LINE channel boasts the shortest conversion time from viewer to member.

![](https://files.readme.io/87c5f43-attribution-report-results.png)

### 

5\. Create a drilldown report for user profiles

You can also create a profile report containing users that satisfy a filter, for example, filtering by conversion channel.

In this example, we’ll select all the 53 users converted from the Google channel by hovering over the cell corresponding to users who converted from Google, and clicking the filter icon. In the generated drilldown report, you can view the profiles of the 53 users who converted from Google.

![](https://files.readme.io/f15c9a0-drilldown.png)   
![](https://files.readme.io/500d625-drilldown-2.png)

### 

6\. Sync user profiles to a third-party platform for retargeting

To sync the profiles in the report to a third-party platform, click the three vertical dots in the top right corner of the drilldown report, click **Sync** , then select the integration you’d like to use.

For example, to [create segments on AIQUA](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/syncing-to-aiqua-segments) for retargeting campaigns, click **Create Segment (AIQUA)**.

![](https://files.readme.io/ea05566-drilldown-2-highlight.png) ![](https://files.readme.io/32cad74-sync-to-aiqua.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

# Identify the most effective acquisition channel

## 

Background

To effectively reach potential users, brands must utilize multiple channels, including email, SMS, paid ads (including Google and Facebook), and social media (including Instagram and LINE). However, data across these channels is often scattered and not consolidated, making it challenging for marketers to evaluate each channel's effectiveness.

### 

Objective

How do I identify the most effective channels to acquire new users?

### 

Challenges

  * Lack of a centralized source for analyzing and comparing acquisition channels.
  * Creating reports manually is time-consuming, and reports must be recreated whenever new data is available.



* * *

## 

Appier's solution

  * **Product** : AIRIS
  * **Feature** : [Attribution](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/attribution) report
    * AIRIS's attribution reports allow marketers to easily quantify the effectiveness of various touchpoints and campaigns leading to conversions. Marketers can then leverage these insights to create segments for further retargeting efforts.
  * **Strengths** :
    * Report data visualizations enable easy analysis of cross-channel acquisition performance.
    * Automatically updated data ensures accurate and up-to-date performance tracking.
    * Segments can be easily leveraged across various integrated platforms for targeted marketing efforts.

![](https://files.readme.io/106650d-target-audience.jpg)

* * *

## 

Setup guide

### 

1\. Create a new attribution report

From navigation sidebar, go to **Analyze** , then click **\+ New Report > Attribution**.

![](https://files.readme.io/110b60573c3763ba7df1116104de826cf08ae179617ea58f5c29b1e58c73c430-use-case-create-attribution-report.png)

### 

2\. Configure report settings

In this example, we want to assess how many website visitors in the past year from each acquisition channel (such as Google, Facebook) have converted into registered members. To do so, we'll configure the following settings:

  * Timeframe
  * Performed by
  * Initial Events
  * Touchpoints
  * Conversion

![](https://files.readme.io/bf0c649708a68829f5b9efe1df21ae94631aff78a32242fb5680efabb0f3b11a-image.png)

#### 

Timeframe

If a user performed at least one event in the specified timeframe, their data will be included in this report. In this example, we’ve selected **1 year ago**.

![](https://files.readme.io/cd92fd4d11a726434ab071317d4b5f67f2255b9e130f81678ccec28e71caaf3d-timeframe.png)

#### 

Performed by

Determines the segment or group of people you'll be analyzing. In this example, we would like to analyze all users in the database, so we’ll select **All users**.

![](https://files.readme.io/03b8e8d0eb6173d0b26a8ae159a56999301fe60b9a15191ef06392e68ee6b24d-performed-by.png)

#### 

Initial Events

Select the first event (or starting point) where you want the attribution analysis to begin within the selected timeframe.

In this example, we would like to include all the users who have ever viewed any page on the website (for example, all the visitors), so we’ll select the **Page view** event without adding any additional constraints.

![](https://files.readme.io/a96579f9b86b2168926c7bd356218996e7351bcbb367a37f1e496974bc546184-initial-events.png)

#### 

Touchpoints

Touchpoints are the specific attribution events that you want to analyze. In this example, we'd like to analyze the difference between each acquisition channel, so we’ve chosen the **UTM campaign** as the touchpoint and selected **Page view** where **Campaign Source = exists** , where the campaign source is where the ad was displayed, for example, Google, Facebook, Instagram.

The dimension determines how the report is organized. For example, since we’ve selected **Campaign Source** as the dimension, the report will display an analysis of all the different campaign sources.

![](https://files.readme.io/6cd3e8127959d14420c517f660894493d9c52f82e55995d800caeb5fcba97b46-touchpoints.png)

#### 

Conversion

This is the conversion event that you want to analyze. In this example, we’ve chosen **Register member** as the conversion event.

![](https://files.readme.io/e5231ab0b53b9fe50e9bb1b5aa5631ef236c581295b41fde791ab02609a892f2-conversion-events.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Developer assistance will be required to log custom events (for example, “Register member”) using the Appier SDK. To learn more, see [Custom Events and Attributes](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/custom-user-data) .

### 

3\. Select an attribution model

Select the attribution model that you want to use. The model determines how credit is assigned for the conversion.

In this example, we’ve selected **Last Touch** , where the last touchpoint receives 100% of the credit for the conversion.

![](https://files.readme.io/cbe0287458c5388a964c8e93b80870bad662990aa0d138c7bb6ee38f2603fa8a-model.png)

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Attribution
> 
> To learn more about the available attribution models, refer to [Attribution report guide](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/attribution#model) .

### 

4\. Run the report

We can see in the report that among all the 224 conversions, `google` was the highest-performing campaign channel, resulting in 62 conversions—meaning users registered as members.

The report also indicates the average time taken from the initial event (page viewed) to conversion (registered as a member). Interestingly, the data reveals that the LINE channel boasts the shortest conversion time from viewer to member.

![](https://files.readme.io/87c5f43-attribution-report-results.png)

### 

5\. Create a drilldown report for user profiles

You can also create a profile report containing users that satisfy a filter, for example, filtering by conversion channel.

In this example, we’ll select all the 53 users converted from the Google channel by hovering over the cell corresponding to users who converted from Google, and clicking the filter icon. In the generated drilldown report, you can view the profiles of the 53 users who converted from Google.

![](https://files.readme.io/f15c9a0-drilldown.png)   
![](https://files.readme.io/500d625-drilldown-2.png)

### 

6\. Sync user profiles to a third-party platform for retargeting

To sync the profiles in the report to a third-party platform, click the three vertical dots in the top right corner of the drilldown report, click **Sync** , then select the integration you’d like to use.

For example, to [create segments on AIQUA](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/syncing-to-aiqua-segments) for retargeting campaigns, click **Create Segment (AIQUA)**.

![](https://files.readme.io/ea05566-drilldown-2-highlight.png) ![](https://files.readme.io/32cad74-sync-to-aiqua.png)

# Condition

Create a segment by specifying user attribute and event conditions.

## 

1\. Add basic segment details

To create a segment using user attribute and event conditions, go to **Segments** and click **\+ Create segment > Conditions**.

![](https://files.readme.io/83e79ce1968112a698aac05001368e4b9125cc88dce4de7a26e77957a87e87b6-create-segment-conditions.png)

Under **Segment info** , enter a name, an optional description, and optional tags.

  * The name will be displayed in the segment list.
  * Tags can be used for filtering in the segment list.

![](https://files.readme.io/161e73cbb228e9e2abdd3f311071a56113819ff8a2543b0bf96c38c66a1efb6d-segment-create-segment-info.png)

* * *

## 

2\. Add segmentation conditions

Under **Configure segment** , click **+** and select an event or attribute to segment users by. Complex segmentation conditions can be combined into condition groups.

  * Event-based conditions include additional settings for constraints, aggregation, and data timeframe.
  * For user attributes and event properties, the available comparison operators differ based on data type.

![](https://files.readme.io/87d2efac122def19292175767b14833e0d551b68754261366b6177f8682fae34-segment-create-configure-segment.png)

### 

Event conditions

Event conditions include additional settings for event properties, aggregations (how events are counted), and the timeframe of events to consider.

![](https://files.readme.io/9901b3c4451dce44cc05c01b13ec7309f775b50cbd15545fdd0f2c6ed8127772-segment-create-event-parameter-aggregation.png)

Setting| Description  
---|---  
**Constraints**|  Constraints are filters for individual event properties. Select an event property from the dropdown, select an operation, and enter the comparison value. The available comparison operators will differ depending on the event property's data type.![](https://files.readme.io/b8deec472f4b4ddb968c24c076ce16e1f851df7921902bbb1f325beaab8b5e78-segment-create-event-parameter-constraint.png)  
**Aggregation**|  The aggregation type determine how events are counted. Select one of the following aggregation types, select an operation, then enter a comparison value.• **Count** : Evaluate the number of times the user has performed the selected event.   
• **Count unique** : Evaluate the number of visits where the user has performed the selected event. If the user performs the action multiple times during a single visit, it's only counted once.   
• **Sum** : Evaluate the sum of the values for numeric event properties, for example, price, purchase quantity, or event duration.![](https://files.readme.io/9901b3c4451dce44cc05c01b13ec7309f775b50cbd15545fdd0f2c6ed8127772-segment-create-event-parameter-aggregation.png)  
**Timeframe**|  Set the range of data that should be used when evaluating this condition. There are three ways to select a timeframe:• **Absolute** : Select a specific date, such as "January 1st, 2023".   
• **Relative** : Select a time that is relative to the present day, such as "90 days ago" or "Today". This timeframe is dynamic and updates in real time.  
• **Annotations** : Use the [annotation date](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/annotations#/) from an existing annotation.  
  
### 

Condition groups

Adding multiple conditions within same level will create a condition group. The following example shows a segment containing users who satisfy both **Condition group A** _and_ **Condition group B** , where:

  * **Condition group A** consists of users who added at least one product to their cart (`product_added_to_cart`) event _or_ viewed a page (`page_viewed`) at least four times in the past two days.
  * **Condition group B** consists of users who haven't made a purchase (`product_purchased`) in the past two days.

![](https://files.readme.io/4da609066d04a184280a759c67417e10d1e4763b169dcc6efaa2693767cf58e2-segment-create-condition-groups.png)

You can perform the different operations to edit segmentation condition groups.

__Create a new condition group

Hover over the condition and click the **+** button that appears inside the condition block.

![](https://files.readme.io/a1557a9bd678401f6f68516f8ae7c252cde46d5533f13aae1b1276401bd63fd5-new-condition-group.png)  


__Add conditions to an existing condition group

Click the **+** button that appears at the end of the condition block.

![](https://files.readme.io/006cf1977b9268266b18cdf33c9bdd9e367e78f98eef9ddf2de607f2c4bddfc4-adding-to-existing-condition-group.png)  


__Set condition group operators

Condition groups are evaluated using the operator specified in the previous (outer) level. Click the operator to switch between **AND** and **OR**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4c236596569db7023b0e7d7e485321f43f832f07825f8a84d009c34e8d0c4a39-operator-switch.gif)  


__Move conditions between groups

Drag and drop individual conditions into the desired condition group.

![](https://files.readme.io/c82fa781cfb0c796d57696f2f13a638dfe84955dd2715740468e571bd669c491-move-conditions.gif)  


__Delete conditions

Hover over the condition and click **X**.

![](https://files.readme.io/c745928579337a7dbb11ed524f5a2d05ad4a2509ccdf5c041c8b32cdeafeabe2-delete-conditions.gif)  


* * *

## 

5\. (Optional) Set profile view settings and review reachable user count

  * Profile view settings: Show the segment in the user's profile view. Only available for accounts with AIRIS enabled.
  * Reachable user counts: View the number of reachable users for each channel. Only available for accounts with AIQUA enabled.



### 

Profile view settings

> 📘
> 
> Profile view settings are only available for accounts with AIRIS enabled.

Check **Show this segment in user profiles** to see the segment name under the **Segments** section of the user's profile for users in this segment.

![](https://files.readme.io/ff331fc93f3e29422bbb52664f8a73864425043a54a9e378c605f720a3abc15e-show-in-user-profiles.png)

### 

Review the reachable user count

> 📘
> 
> Reachable user counts are only available for accounts with AIQUA enabled.

Under **Segment users** , you can review the segment's reachable user count broken down by channel.

![](https://files.readme.io/7318c2d4ff2e44e3f4fba6a9642f55bd7137d2ef6ce25a878125c17719911f42-segment-users.png)

* * *

## 

6\. Save the segment

Click **Create** to finish save the segment. Next, learn how to manage your segment and export it to a third-party ad platform.

[ ![](https://files.readme.io/9acf32098df5308f60771dbc7699eaeeaaee8008a689800cefbc77ff889cb9e0-manage-icon.png) Manage segments View details and perform segment operations ](/docs/segments-manage) [ ![](https://files.readme.io/b520681193e51940b0f4cb6e96073bb59eb8d69c20ee50404bb2c8c3e60b7614-nameexport_styleoutlined_size24.png) Export to ad platforms Export segments to third-party ad platforms ](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/manage-segments-export-a-segment)

* * *

## 

Comparison operators

Different comparison operators are available depending on the data type of the user attribute or event property.

Boolean Text Number Timestamp

### 

Boolean

Compare the boolean attribute value directly. The condition evaluates to true when the attribute matches the selected value (**true** or **false**).

### 

Number

Operator| Description  
---|---  
`equals`| Evaluates to true if the number matches the specified value.  
`greater than`| Evaluates to true if the number is greater than the specified value.  
`less than`| Evaluates to true if the number is less than the specified value.  
`greater than or equals`| Evaluates to true if the number is greater than or equal to the specified value.  
`less than or equals`| Evaluates to true if the number is less than or equal to the specified value.  
`does not equal`| Evaluates to true if the number doesn't match the specified value.  
  
### 

Text

Operator| Description  
---|---  
`is`| Evaluates to true if the text exactly matches the specified string (case-sensitive).  
`contains`| Evaluates to true if the text includes the specified substring.  
`exists`| Evaluates to true if the attribute has any non-empty value.  
`starts with`| Evaluates to true if the text begins with the specified string.  
`ends with`| Evaluates to true if the text ends with the specified string.  
`is not`| Evaluates to true if the text doesn't exactly match the specified string.  
  
### 

Timestamp

There are several types of timestamp data, each with different operators.

Timestamp type| Description  
---|---  
**Timestamp**|  Supports standard numerical comparisons.  
**Absolute date**|  Supports comparisons based on an absolute date. Supports the **Ignore year** option.  
**Relative date**|  Supports comparisons relative to the current date. Supports the **Ignore year** option.  
**Calendar month**|  Supported comparisons based on the month. Supports the **Ignore year** option.  
  
#### 

Timestamp: Timestamp

Operator| Description  
---|---  
`after`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs after the specified date and time.  
`before`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs before the specified date and time.  
`on or after`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs on or after the specified date and time.  
`on or before`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs on or before the specified date and time.  
`exists`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp attribute has any recorded value.  
`does not exist`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp attribute has no recorded value.  
  
#### 

Timestamp: Absolute date

Operator| Description  
---|---  
`on`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs any time on the specified calendar date.  
`on or before`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs on or before the specified calendar date.  
`on or after`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs on or after the specified calendar date.  
`between`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp falls within the specified date range, inclusive of both start and end dates.  
  
#### 

Timestamp: Relative

Operator| Description  
---|---  
`Today`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp falls on the current calendar day.  
`In the last`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurred within the specified number of days/weeks/months before the current date.  
`In the next`| Evaluates to true if timestamp occurs within the specified number of days/weeks/months from the current date.  
`More than ... ago`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurred more than the specified time period before the current date.  
`More than ... later`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs more than the specified time period after the current date.  
`Exactly ... ago`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurred at exactly the specified time period from the current date.  
`Exactly ... later`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp occurs at exactly the specified time period after the current date.  
  
#### 

Timestamp: Calendar month

Operator| Description  
---|---  
`This month`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp falls within the current calendar month.  
`Last month`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp falls within the previous calendar month.  
`Next month`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp falls within the next calendar month.  
`In the month of`| Evaluates to true if the timestamp falls within the specified month.  
  
#### 

**Ignore year** option (annually recurring events)

Check the **Ignore year** option to capture annually recurring events, such as birthdays or membership anniversaries. This option is available for the following timestamp types:

  * **Absolute date**
  * **Relative**
  * **Calendar month**

![](https://files.readme.io/d3765574f46f32badf2f43709e0828b96f25631b3d4dc5112418a1c70a2bb816-segment-ignore-year.png)

# Split Segment

Split an existing segment into smaller sub-segments.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Split segments are [static](doc:segments-getting-started#static-segments) and independent of their source segment.
> 
>   * Once created, users can't be added to or removed from split segments.
>   * Split segments aren't affected by changes to their source segment.
> 


  1. Select a source segment
  2. Choose how to divide users
  3. Add split segments and allocate users
  4. Finish creating the split segment group



* * *

## 

1\. Select a source segment

In the segment list, find the segment you want to split and click ![](https://files.readme.io/1e1263152ae6c7f19c05af715e10d3dd663439ce5ccb78e4f746c504c3df5cf7-more-options-icon.png) **> Split users**. 

![](https://files.readme.io/14b3707c7f9e9cf8c442cf3b1058cbf93676d4547ed2e895877047dcaccd8ed2-segment-create-split-users.png)   


* * *

## 

2\. Choose how to divide users

Choose how to divide users into each split segment.

  * **Number** : Enter the number of users to add in each segment.
  * **Percentage** : Enter the percentage of users from the source segment to add in each segment.

![](https://files.readme.io/c0011fe0cc93d4905d28b09860e4129dfdb962dcdb020383da9d89659dc10e32-split-segment-split-by.png)

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Review user counts
> 
> Click **User counts by platform** to view the number of users in the source segment by platform.
> 
> ![](https://files.readme.io/700a5f88fec13fdc575ece0016f4e8a8459dfe130fc9100411b0173e1c939734-split-segment-user-counts-by-platform.png)

* * *

## 

3\. Add split segments and allocate users

  1. Click **\+ Add split segment** to add a new split segment. You can add up to 10 segments.
  2. Enter the number or percentage of users to allocate to each split segment. To divide users evenly across all segments, click **Divide equally**.

![](https://files.readme.io/a6ae37ee1f8d98ed52d4a4c24ce84fb55077376b7e02b74342810429a959a393-split-segment-add-divide.png)

* * *

## 

4\. Finish creating the split segment group

Enter a name for the split segment group, then click **Create**.

![](https://files.readme.io/e4af2cc1a972b2f02e30999693abb2709137fdb57483fc82490bb7439f7707bf-split-segment-name.png)

In the segment list, a new group containing all the split segments will appear.

![](https://files.readme.io/93068c33c3e277d2ffeb2a3cb9400def9a9b2e8913d0e32eea3d71f824ad050a-segments-split-group.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Deleting split segment groups
> 
> You can delete the entire split segment group, but individual split segments can't be deleted.

__Updated 21 days ago

* * *

# User List

Create a segment by uploading a CSV file containing user IDs to map with existing users.

## 

1\. Add basic segment details

To create a segment by mapping a list of user IDs in an uploaded CSV file, go to **Segments** and click **\+ Create segment > User list**.

![](https://files.readme.io/51b71384bd8446b7a52f80f44655d5f600594aca7db835a33d563f0ccdf7b773-segments-create-user-list.png)

Under **Segment info** , enter a name, an optional description, and optional tags.

  * The name will be displayed in the segment list.
  * Tags can be used for searching and filtering in the segment list.

![](https://files.readme.io/c8b94eee487d19c2c9a4df274b9a7b673a70a6e5f1bb01cceee6ba6ab4d11a20-segments-create-user-list-info.png)

* * *

## 

2\. Upload a CSV file

Under **Source CSV** , choose whether upload a new user file or select an existing one. CSV files can be up to 50 MB.

![](https://files.readme.io/e8aea33e2ae711337f788bd1b76ad1dab21220e9920ec225fb2980f1dd471274-segments-create-user-list-csv.png)

* * *

## 

3\. Select the primary identifier

Under **Map primary identifier** , map the CSV column containing the primary user ID to the corresponding attribute in your project's existing database.

> 🚧
> 
> ### 
> 
> Unmatched user IDs
> 
> If a user ID from the source CSV column doesn't match any existing users in your project's database, that ID is ignored. The user won't be included in the segment.

![](https://files.readme.io/4cf85a10d6e4ec322fac7952f4c1742849f29831e5bbfdde1366a74bbf708cd9-segments-create-user-list-map.png)

* * *

## 

4\. Save the segment

Click **Create** to finish save the segment. Next, learn how to manage your segment and export it to a third-party ad platform.

[ ![](https://files.readme.io/9acf32098df5308f60771dbc7699eaeeaaee8008a689800cefbc77ff889cb9e0-manage-icon.png) Manage segments View details and perform segment operations ](/docs/segments-manage) [ ![](https://files.readme.io/b520681193e51940b0f4cb6e96073bb59eb8d69c20ee50404bb2c8c3e60b7614-nameexport_styleoutlined_size24.png) Export to ad platforms Export segments to third-party ad platforms ](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/manage-segments-export-a-segment)

# Getting Started with Segments

Use audience segments for targeted marketing campaigns and reports.

Audience segments are groups of customers categorized by shared attributes, such as demographic data, purchase history, or behavioral patterns. Use segments to deliver personalized marketing campaigns and generate detailed reports to gain insights into your audience.

> 👍
> 
> Unified segments are shared by AIQUA and AIRIS instances within the same project, making them accessible from both products.

[ ![](https://files.readme.io/f501633cc6b7a3f90a31389732e377fdea0a4d9bfc2258354625c2e61fed1dcf-nameaudience_stylesolid_size24.png) Create a segment Create a segment via condition, user list, or splitting. ](/docs/segments-create) [ ![](https://files.readme.io/9acf32098df5308f60771dbc7699eaeeaaee8008a689800cefbc77ff889cb9e0-manage-icon.png) Manage segments View details and perform segment operations. ](/docs/segments-manage) [ ![](https://files.readme.io/b520681193e51940b0f4cb6e96073bb59eb8d69c20ee50404bb2c8c3e60b7614-nameexport_styleoutlined_size24.png) Export to ad platforms Export segments to third-party ad platforms. ](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/manage-segments-export-a-segment)

* * *

## 

Notes and limitations

Segments can contain up to five million users. If you need larger segments, please contact your customer success manager.

* * *

## 

Segment sources and segment types

The segment type determines whether a segment is refreshed automatically or not, and the segment source describes how the segment was created. Both factors determine how and when a segment is refreshed.

### 

Segment types

There are two types of segments with different refresh behaviors.

  * Dynamic segments: Recently active dynamic segments are refreshed automatically.
  * Static segments: Static segments don't refresh automatically. Refresh behavior is determined by the segment source.

![](https://files.readme.io/ebb0ec9d4b04c4b087002ff7408b62743a660886a4d91b74aee6a837445e9437-segments-segment-type.png)

#### 

Dynamic segments

Segment source| Refresh behavior  
---|---  
• **Default**  
• **Conditions**|  The 50 most recently active segments within the past seven days are automatically refreshed at midnight each day, based on your project's timezone.Segments are active if they were created, edited, or used in a campaign within the past seven days.  
  
#### 

Static segments

Static segments aren't refreshed automatically.

Segment source| Refresh behavior  
---|---  
**User list**|  The segment is refreshed when a new source file is uploaded or selected.  
**Split user**|  The segment can't be refreshed after creation.  
  
### 

Segment sources

Segment source| Description  
---|---  
**Default**|  Only available for projects with AIQUA enabled. [**Test Segment**](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/docs/segments-test-segment) is a default segment that exists for all projects by default. This segment is used for sending test notifications during campaign creation.  
**Condition**| [Segments created by specifying segmentation conditions](doc:segments-create-condition). Unified segments are synced between AIQUA and AIRIS instances within the same project.  
**User list**|  Segments created by [uploading a CSV file](doc:segments-create-user-list) with a list of user identifiers. The segment only contains users whose identifiers match those of existing users in your project's database.  
**AIQUA**|  Non-unified segments that can only be used in AIQUA campaigns. These segments aren't available for AIRIS features.
